Backlog Run Decisions

Running record of every non-trivial decision made while the coordinator session drove the planned-plans backlog toward working app prototypes, starting 2026-10-17 from main 5c48916c1; slices 1-22 landed as PRs #133-#162. Each slice ran in its own worktree from the latest tip, with a workflow, commit, PR, Auto-fix and a Codex Sol 6.1 xhigh adversarial review. Newest entries last.

Decisions

D-01 Slice sessions run on Opus 5.5; the coordinator stays on Fable

When
2026-10-17, before slice 1
Context
Usage at start: 5-hour window 58% (resets in 49 min), weekly all-models 83%, weekly Fable 96% (both weekly windows reset in 3 d 14 h). The user asked to run to 100% and reset.
Decision
Start every slice session with model: opus so implementation work spends the all-models window rather than the 4% of Fable headroom. Coordination, verification and this record stay on Fable. Codex Sol 6.1 xhigh reviews are outside this budget and are used at every PR.
Why
A Fable weekly lockout lasts three and a half days; an Opus slice plus a Codex adversarial review keeps the PR quality bar while getting more backlog through before any reset.
Reversal
If the user resets the weekly windows, later slices may inherit Fable again.

D-02 "All the apps" means the Treehouse shell; Township and Toolshed stay frozen

When
2026-10-17, before slice 1
Context
Working policy rule 10 (plans/README.md) freezes Township and Toolshed until R26. The unified roadmap is the only execution ledger (rule 12).
Decision
Slices are chosen by whether they change what a user sees or can do in the Treehouse shell, or unblock a roadmap row on the path to R13/R14. Parity ladders (rows 211-215) are taken only when nothing shell-visible is ready; rule 9 caps ladders at three.

D-03 This record lives in the repo and is committed at checkpoints, not on main directly

When
2026-10-17
Decision
Path plans/decisions-2026-10-17-backlog-run.html. It was kept untracked in the main checkout through slice 7, then landed in its own small PR at the user's request on 2026-10-18. After that, the coordinator batches new entries into periodic ledger PRs; slices never edit it.

D-04 Unmerged leftover branches are inputs to slice planning, not automatic work

When
2026-10-17
Context
claude/r11b-activation-crash-windows (2 commits, 9 files, no PR: activation bound to the controlled boot, lost-commit reconciliation) and claude/packaged-sqlite-busy-retry (3 commits, superseded by merged PR #119) sit unmerged.
Decision
Each slice session is told these exist and may salvage the R11b branch if its planning step picks R11b; the sqlite branch is treated as superseded and left for branch cleanup.

D-05 The coordinator merges a slice PR once its exact tip is green and its review threads are closed

When
2026-10-17, after slice 1
Context
Each slice is cut "from the latest tip". Leaving PRs open would cut every later slice from the same stale main and stack conflicts.
Decision
Merge with a merge commit (the repo's existing style) when working policy rule 2 holds: the exact-tip hosted run is green and the branch fast-forwards from main, or a merge-result run is green. Every review thread must be fixed or rejected with one line first. The slice session itself never merges.

D-06 Slice 1 decisions (rows 199/200)

Scope
Rows 199 and 200 together, no plan file; the D3 snapshot is cached on the identity of the saved state plus key availability.
Gating
Configure routes, Issue invitation, Admit and grant and Save edit now require live authority as well as a key; the matching actions check the same facts.
Hardening
The invitation reuse path and an already-admitted admitAndGrant replay now refuse with no_capability without the capability, so button and action agree.
Rule 6
Source-text pins on computed bodies were removed after Codex flagged them; message tests pin key presence only.
Usage
Weekly Fable reached 100% during slice 1; the coordinator itself moved to Opus 5.5 at the user's switch.

D-07 Slice 2 decisions (R11b, row 216)

Scope
Salvaged claude/r11b-activation-crash-windows and closed PR #126 residuals 1 and 2; residuals 3-5 filed as rows 217-219. Roadmap R11b moves PLANNED to IN PROGRESS; ready/catalogued/listed and R11c stay open.
Review
Two reviews ran although the paths are outside rule 3, following PR #126's precedent for operator contract changes.
Rejected
Moving boot_guard before do_prepare (the deployment term is cleared on refusal) and binding responses to plan kind (the helper is pinned to the same checkout).
Fail-closed
A guard-refused boot stays down until an operator acts; a not-yet-existing log under a symlinked parent is an accepted residual.

D-08 Overlap slices when their file sets are disjoint

When
2026-10-17, while PR #134's hosted CI ran
Decision
Start the next slice before the previous PR merges when the next slice stays out of the previous PR's files (here apps/lattice_carrier_server, config/runtime.exs, the operator script). Both may touch plans/README.md index rows, which merge line-by-line. The later PR is synced with main before its own merge.
Why
The coordinator is only woken by slice reports, not by CI success, so waiting idle on CI wastes the run.

D-09 New slices start only on a user turn

When
2026-10-17, after slice 2 reported
Context
The desktop app refuses to start a session on a turn that a session report triggered. The coordinator can verify and merge on those turns, but it cannot dispatch the next slice.
Decision
On a report turn the coordinator verifies, merges when green, and leaves the next slice as a card. Any user message ("go", "continue") starts it. Slice 3 waited as card task_451ec195 until the user said go.

D-10 Slice 3 decisions (rows 207, 210, plan 186)

207
Overturns the PR #127 "by design" ruling: an unrepresentable call result is answered with a 2-tuple {:response, ref} inbox op, and await/3 returns {:error, :unrepresentable_result}. The wire vocabulary is unchanged.
210
The cutoff atom set is derived at compile time from CatalogCutoff; the source-regex pin is replaced by a behavioural probe and a runtime vocabulary check (rule 6).
186
Design only (305 lines). Three move PRs behind a two-interface host port. Treehouse is the only consumer while rule 10 freezes Township, so the plan carries a fold-back condition.
Carrier limit
CodeRabbit found that the fallback response reaches only peers that already hold the off-vocabulary op, because a checked carrier pull refuses that op's page whatever the response depends on (row 208). The slice narrowed the Registry docs to say so rather than change dependencies; row 208 stays the owner.
Rejected
Nondeterministic find_response when a ref holds both response shapes: pre-existing, needs a second signer.

D-11 Index status cells for merged slices are corrected in one closing ledger PR

When
2026-10-17, after slice 2 noted row 216 still reads IN PROGRESS with the PR cited as open
Decision
Rather than a follow-up PR per row, stale status cells (row 216 and any later ones) land together with this decisions record in one ledger PR at the end of the run, or earlier if a slice's own diff touches the same rows.

D-12 PR #135's red runs are treated as flakes and rerun, not reverted

When
2026-10-17, after #135 landed
Evidence
Tip run 39064519203 failed only the packaged macOS enrollment job ("timed out waiting for the founder to receive the joiner post by hint"). Main run 39064793571 on the same tree passed that job and failed only the Android emulator launch, because Google's system-image download returned "File not found". #135 touched only lattice_core Registry/CatalogTrust code and plans.
Decision
Rerun main's failed jobs instead of reverting. If the enrollment hint timeout recurs on an unrelated PR, it gets its own row as a packaged-gate flake.

D-13 Slice 4 decisions (plan 186 PR 1)

Peer only
lattice-client is a kit peerDependency, not also a dev dependency: a kit-local copy under --preserve-symlinks split instanceof. The kit typechecks through a tsconfig paths entry, and .npmrc sets legacy-peer-deps.
Subpath
BEAM helpers ship on a node-only ./testing/beam subpath, so pinned enrollment scripts stay unchanged.
Guards
A Vite build plugin rejects kit testing code in the production module graph; the kit check rejects any compiled file outside the kit or lattice-client; a runtime wiring check asserts one CarrierPeerError identity.
Deferred
CI does not yet install or check the kit, and cache keys omit it: row 220, required before PR 2. Vite preserveSymlinks is deferred to PR 2.
Lockfile
Edited by hand and verified with npm ci, because npm 10.9 and 11 both strip the existing libc fields.

D-14 After the weekly reset, slices inherit the coordinator's model

When
2026-10-17, during slice 4: weekly Fable 6%, all-models 8%, 5-hour 31%
Decision
D-01's Opus override is dropped. Slices now inherit the coordinator's model, which the user set to Opus 5.5 with /model. The run continues until a window reaches 100%, then the coordinator sends a notification.

D-15 Row 221: re-pin packaged:enrollment to tsx --preserve-symlinks

When
2026-10-17, after slice 5 stopped plan 186 PR 2 at commit 1 on this operator question
Options
(a) re-pin the script in enrollment_ci_contract.mjs with a documented procedure; (b) inject lattice-client values into the kit (plan 186 decision 4c); (c) set NODE_PRESERVE_SYMLINKS=1 in the job env.
Decision
(a). The contract already carries a precedent: plan 183's dated re-pin note on 2026-10-08 ("every pin below stayed exact; none was deleted or loosened. What moved, and why"). Slice 6 changes the pinned string to tsx --preserve-symlinks test/packaged_enrollment.ts, adds a dated plan 186 re-pin note in the same form, and keeps every other pin exact. Every other kit-consuming test script already runs that way.
Why not (b) or (c)
(b) changes the kit's architecture to work around a test pin. (c) is unverified and global to the job, so it would also change how the digest-pinned steps resolve modules.

D-16 Slice hand-over notes are an input to every later brief

When
2026-10-17, at the user's prompt, during slice 6
Context
Each slice leaves a hand-over note in the app's shared notes folder for this session group (slices 2 to 5 so far). The coordinator had not been reading them. They match the PR bodies but add toolchain traps.
Traps recorded
Background codex exec waits on stdin, so append < /dev/null. A fresh worktree needs npm ci in lattice-client and the Treehouse shell with Node 22, or about ten Treehouse parity tests fail with :enoent on tsx. A kit-local lattice-client copy splits instanceof. npm 10.9 and 11 strip lockfile libc fields. A renamed branch is not picked up by the PR bar. The Linux-only operator tests run in Docker elixir:1.19.5-otp-28 with umask 022.
Decision
The coordinator reads new hand-over notes on every slice report, forwards traps to the running slice, and puts them in the next brief. The traps above went to slice 6 and into project memory.

D-17 Slice 6 decisions (plan 186 PR 2, row 221)

Subpath
Carrier modules export on ./sync; the . barrel stays routes-only so unflagged tsx tests still resolve.
Port
The signer and both route validators are members of the two-interface port rather than a third interface. Space-first ordering is an isAnchor predicate, plus holdsAnchorProfile for the feed's Space pre-sync.
Product copy
Status copy and feed env flags stay in the shell; the kit's panel sync takes describe, pollMs and autosyncOnMount.
Re-pin
As D-15, plus gate:enrollment also gains --preserve-symlinks, because it imports the moved modules and nothing pins it.
Live options
Adapters forward the caller's options through a Proxy so they are read live, as the old modules read them.
Flake
test/feed.ts:233's fixed 30 ms quiet window fails under host load above 500, equally at base and head; filed as row 222.
Coordinator
The Proxy fix landed after both reviews, so the coordinator asked for a Codex confirm pass. It found three more P2s in the same class: the receiver for getters and callbacks, frozen options with a host key, and read order. All three were fixed in d91217f8b, and the full chain passed. Per rule 3 there is no third round. If a later review finds the same live-options class again, the Proxy adapter becomes a design row: replace it with call-time option reads in the kit.

D-18 After plan 186: the enrollment path, with the record landed separately

When
2026-10-18, after slice 7; usage weekly all-models 47%, Fable 6%
Options offered
(1) R11b remainder toward R11c and R13; (2) test and CI hygiene, rows 222, 202 and 201; (3) design rows 187-189; (4) the parity ladder, rows 211-215; (5) land this record.
Decision (user)
Option 1, with row 222 folded into slice 8, and option 5 as its own small PR. The same PR sets plan 186 and index row 186 to DONE, the only status cell left stale after this run's merges.

D-19 Slice 8 decisions, and the enrollment path's rule 8 wall

217
Activation re-reviews the roster from the sealed Space log and refuses :roster_drift_since_staging, with no journal schema change. A later drift, between activation and restart, is a distinct atom in the ready phase.
218
The owner appends each replaced journal to a reserved, private operator-journal-history.jsonl. A torn tail is cut back to whole lines, and a failed write refuses. Unbounded growth is row 225.
219
The candidate takes the replaced manifest's mode, masked to 0o777. A group- or world-writable manifest is refused.
222
feed.ts waits on the drained and generation conditions; it keeps the quiet windows that guard the "exactly one more" negative assertions.
Wall
Plan 223 makes ready the next slice (row 223). catalogued and listed (row 224) are BLOCKED under rule 8 on the operator catalog signer, the root bootstrap binding and a verified signer backup. R11c needs catalogued, so R11c and R13 cannot start in this run without those operator inputs.

D-20 Plan 223's probe key: a roster member's transport identity, never a root or service key

When
2026-10-18, before slice 9
Context
Plan 223 STOPs if the operator holds no key in the admitted instance's trusted_peers for the readiness frontier pull. The set is {child log root} ∪ roster.
Decision
The probe authenticates as a roster member whose transport identity the operator names by file, as plan 223 already specifies. It never uses the child log root's signing key or the carrier service key, so the roadmap's key-purpose separation holds. If the deployment model gives the operator no member transport key, slice 9 does not invent one: it parks row 223 with that finding and takes the next unblocked row.

D-21 Row 223 parked: no operator-held key may authenticate the readiness probe

When
2026-10-18, slice 9
Finding
The admitted instance's trusted_peers is pinned to exactly {child log root} ∪ roster (staging.ex:409), and the carrier admits only realms in that map (web_socket.ex:112). D-20 bars the root and service keys. Member keys are device-held, and the catalog lifecycle research says the operator does not sign as a member. A dedicated probe realm is plan 223 STOP 2; widening the exact-peer check is STOP 3.
Decision
Slice 9 parked row 223 and took row 201 instead. The enrollment path (R11b ready, then row 224, R11c and R13) now waits on operator decisions, which go to the user.

D-22 Operator decision: row 223 takes option (a), a read-only operator probe realm

When
2026-10-18, decided by the user as operator
Decision
The readiness frontier pull authenticates through a read-only operator probe realm on the carrier server, usable for frontier and missing-op pulls only. Plan 223 is refreshed and the phase is built as slice 10. This supersedes D-20's roster-member requirement for the probe, and lifts plan 223 STOP 2 and 3 for this purpose only.
Constraints kept
The probe key is named by file and kept distinct from the child root, the service key and every member key. It never joins trusted_peers or the roster, and cannot submit, relay or subscribe. Its seed lives only in that operator-owned file (mode 0600). It is read for one session and never copied into the journal, a plan, the manifest, telemetry or logs. Activation stays local-only (D1). Two independent adversarial reviews are required, one of them security-focused.

D-23 Row 202 and Dependabot run in parallel with slice 10; Township security bumps are allowed

When
2026-10-18
Ordering
The user said row 202 and Dependabot come "then", after slice 10. Their files share nothing with the carrier server, so they run as slice 11 alongside it (D-08). Slice 10 still merges on its own gate.
Township
Alert #36 (high, @vue/server-renderer XSS, fixed in 3.5.42) is in frozen Township's lockfile. A security-only lockfile bump is maintenance, not the new product work rule 10 forbids.
glib
Alert #13 (medium, glib < 0.20 unsoundness) is likely held back by Tauri 2's GTK3 stack. The slice does not leave the supported Tauri line to force it. It checks whether the vulnerable API is reachable, and the coordinator dismisses the alert with that stated reason if it cannot be bumped.

D-24 Dependabot #13 (glib) dismissed as not used; a lattice_stress restart timeout is a flake

When
2026-10-18, slice 11
glib
No upgrade path exists: wry 0.57, tao 0.37, tauri 2.12 and webkit2gtk 2.0 all require gtk/glib ^0.18, and glib 0.20 ships only on gtk4-rs. VariantStrIter is built only by Variant::array_iter_str, which nothing in the lockfile or src-tauri calls, and glib is built only for Linux. The coordinator dismissed alert #13 as not_used with that reason. It can be reopened when Tauri moves to GTK4.
#36
Township's lockfile moves vue and every @vue/* package to 3.5.44, a lockfile-only change with every libc field preserved. Treehouse already resolved 3.5.43.
Flake
On an earlier #143 tip, apps/lattice_stress failure_semantics_test.exs:41 timed out waiting for a crashed topology process to restart, taking 38 of 52 tests down with it. The PR changes no Elixir code, and main was green on the same suite. If it recurs on another PR, it gets its own row.

D-26 Slice 10 decisions (probe realm and ready)

Grant
The probe grant is ephemeral and single-use: one public key per instance, a 10 s lifetime, consumed by an exact-object ETS delete after the challenge verifies, and revoked in an after block. It adds no manifest field and no standing credential.
Realm
lattice-operator-probe is checked only after a trusted_peers miss. A probe session may send only frontier and pull, and everything else gets read_only without reaching the Holder. The session closes 30 s after authentication; a very large log can hit that cap, which fails closed and is row 226.
Key
The operator names the key file at call time. It is refused if it equals any service key, any trusted_peers key, or the Space or child authority root.

D-29 Slice 13 decisions, and the row 232 gap behind the ceremony

Crypto
The backup uses PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 (600,000 iterations) and AES-256-GCM, with a sorted-key header as AAD. Passphrases are printable ASCII only, so an independent Python recipe, which needs nothing from the repo, can recover the key.
Custody
The tool refuses a terminal stdin for passphrases, because System.cmd stty has no controlling tty; the runbook reads them silently in the shell. It canonicalizes paths once, validates every ancestor's ownership, creates files raw O_EXCL, refuses disposable keys in real mode, never signs in real mode, and requires the restore drill on a second host. Erlang's lack of fchmod and openat is the accepted residual after two rounds.
Gap
The Treehouse app creates legacy-family Spaces without the R04 bounded profile, and it has no command that authors catalog_bootstrap_v1, so the living root cannot sign the binding. The tool refuses such a Space (space_not_bounded_profile). The report records the signer and the verified backup with binding: pending_root_signature. Row 232 owns the fix, which is custody code under src-tauri and needs a rule 3 second review.
Order
The operator can run the signer and backup parts of the ceremony now. The binding part follows row 232.

Family
New Spaces use replica:treehouse:space:<nonce>#authority:bounded-continuation-v1#root:<tag>, with genesis ops for the six preview commands plus catalog_bootstrap_v1, attest_member_key_v1 and replace_catalog_v1. Threads stay legacy. Legacy Spaces still load, cannot be migrated in place, and are refused for the pin and the binding.

D-41 Slice 20: the catalog store enforces its own chain rule (D-38 exception)

When
2026-10-23, on frozen PR #158
Finding
CodeRabbit showed Lock.catalogued/6 checked an envelope's id, digest, Space and path, but not revision or previous against the committed head. A caller could store a second revision-0 chain, or an envelope not extending the head. Every later boot would then refuse until manual repair. No caller exists yet: PR 4, the only planned caller, is not built and is designed to check the chain before calling.
Decision
This is the D-38 exception for a write the store should refuse, so it was fixed on the frozen branch. Lock.catalog_artifact/3 runs CatalogStore.verify, requires no different pending envelope, previous equal to the head, and revision one above it (0 with no head), and otherwise refuses catalog_artifact_mismatch. Three tests show each refusal leaves journal, history and store unchanged. The BEAM write path now enforces the chain itself, whatever any future caller checks.
Residual
A direct call to the Python owner, bypassing the BEAM, skips the check. That path already trusted its caller, because the owner cannot decode envelopes.
Next
Plan 224 PRs 1 and 3 are merged. PR 2 (realm, routes, relay, probe per route) can start. PRs 4 and 5 wait on the operator's ceremony report.

D-42 Slice 21: staging refuses one replica served twice (D-38 exception)

When
2026-10-23/24, on frozen PR #160
Finding
The Codex bot showed check_bound kept one authority root per replica. If two served Treehouse logs declared the same replica with different roots, one root dropped out of the service-key-reuse inventory, so a route's root could equal the service key. That is plan 224's STOP class. A bound replica commits to its root, so only a hand-configured, unbound legacy Thread served twice reaches it.
Decision
This is a missing refusal, so it was fixed on the frozen branch. check_bound refuses :service_binding_mismatch when two instances serve one replica. Each instance serves one log, so this also covers differing roots, in staging, activation and readiness.
Deferred
A second Codex finding said the staging catalog check runs before the owner lock. The owner already refuses stale_catalog_head under its lock when an envelope is pending, so the earlier check is defense in depth, not a gap. No row was filed.
State
Plan 224 PRs 1, 2 and 3 are built and merged. PRs 4 (catalogued) and 5 (listed) wait on the operator's ceremony report, and PR 6 (client acceptance) needs its own plan (row 245).

OD-7
(a) as amended. initialize() writes an independent initialised marker under identity_lock, then the sentinel, then the identity last. With an identity record present, a missing or corrupt trust record beside the marker is trust_recovery_required, with no recreation; before the identity exists, every crash boundary in initialize() retries. Identities that predate the build get a one-time confirmed migration (a nonclaim), which writes the sentinel before the marker so a crash between them stays retryable.

D-46 Offline enrollment and guarded initial provisioning

Decision
Plan 262 preserves ordinary atomic Thread creation. Introduce an explicit initial_existing reference observation only for a first one-Space/one-Thread publication, with a verified empty catalog store under the mutation owner's lock. Pending mode retains absence semantics. Catalog/listing refuse the new mode until their actual guarded consumers exist.
Trust and order
Use the existing Space pin/export, operator binding-payload, ordinary Catalog binding review/sign, re-export and verify-binding/report ceremony before the offline invitation. Confirm the recipient key independently on the founder side. The invitation carries Space evidence only; final provisioning carries both histories. Candidate review installs no trust or routes.
State
The reference mode is journal metadata, checked against exact signed history presence in every phase. Both journal twins encode it explicitly. Complete deletion of all operator state cannot be distinguished from first use; preserving that state is an operator trust assumption. Older binaries cannot read new-mode history, so rollback needs a compatible forward fix. New output precedes the operator root's first controlled boot; a prior boot marker refuses another fresh import. Completed retries only verify/sync exact existing files. This is not a process-liveness detector or permission to reset state.
Review refinements
Keep strict clean-history checks for initial import and unserved staging; live rechecks ignore quarantined evidence while checking exact effective authority and permitting ordinary monitoring. Preserve a sealed public bundle and provide a read-only staging-input reload after live append-only Space growth or activation. Require nonzero distinct ordinary CLI ports before creating output. Reject mismatched parent/child titles, service/member key reuse and tombstoned Spaces. Postwrite reread checks and partial-output refusal remain intentional; do not remove checks or delete recovery evidence to make retry succeed.
Parent merge and picker evidence

The AX snapshot then showed the HTML file input's inner button named no file selected. No complete packaged proof is claimed yet.

D-67 Treat cutoff proofs as complete recovery points

Decision
A malformed closed proof envelope or cutoff is malformed_catalog. An unreadable nested proof history is recovery_incomplete with no ancestor-fetch ids; a readable but mismatched cutoff or membership retains the claimed digest diagnostic. Corrupt saved proof state requires trust_recovery_required without fetch ids before any incoming repair.
Reason
A cutoff proof claims a complete immutable point. Its missing ancestors, unsupported vocabulary and bad signatures cannot become incremental fetch requests. Main-history observation retains its separate refusal order. No signature, cutoff, portability or subset check is bypassed.
Evidence and scope
Fresh TypeScript producer reproduced unsupported_cutoff versus BEAM recovery_incomplete before the change. New reciprocal producers exercise valid two-route installations, unsupported, forged, partial and malformed histories, malformed envelopes, mismatched digests and saved-proof corruption. Independent review added a valid strict-prefix proof and a readable correctly digested proof containing an authentic absent member; these distinguish subset acceptance from membership refusal. TypeScript source maps the complete-proof context and clears fetch diagnostics when rejecting corrupt saved state; a fresh wrong-digest saved-proof regression demonstrated the prior diagnostic mismatch. Final reciprocal checks and reviews remain required.

D-130 Row 257: keep the 1 MiB record; pause at full; owner check folded into plan 245

2026-10-31

D-131 Row 257 feed design: a host capacity reason, per-route pause, dependent pause, Sync now as the only retry

When
2026-10-31
Context
The kit's feed is product-neutral, and the store is one record shared by every route, so a capacity stop is a store fact seen through whichever route merges.
Options
(a) Hard-code preview_storage_limit in the kit; (b) an optional ShellHost.capacityReason; (c) a global pause of every route.
Decision
(b), per route. A route whose merge fails with the reason is marked full and its worker is cancelled; queued syncs, hints, polls and Space-triggered Thread syncs skip it. A Thread that must sync its Space first pauses as dependent and never retries a paused Space. Sync now restarts paused workers; reconfigure clears the pause. The two-device gate is exact to 4 bytes, because post text is stored as base64; the byte-exact cap is gated by a whitespace-padded record in TS and native.
Reason
(c) would stop a route whose next pull still fits. A route paused per reason keeps the kit free of Treehouse names and needs no new error vocabulary.

D-135 Row 289: refuse a non-MapSet at valid_sig?/1; do not coerce

When
2026-10-31
Context
Canonical.delegation_bytes/8 sorts a list or a MapSet of the same names into identical bytes, so a grant whose ops or roles is a list still verifies. Canonical bytes, wire, and Op.valid?/1 stay unchanged. This is not a receipt-free W4 claim, not Phase G, and not a new authoring surface.
Reason
Coercing would honor a second in-memory shape the constructor never emits and hide the same log from every other reader that still expects a MapSet. Catching in Verdict would leave Sim, reduction, and live paths crashing, which D-142 already rejected.

D-176 Backlog run 2026-11-01: coordinator, monitor and merge policy

Context

Options
Per-slice Workflows versus ad hoc agents.
Decision
Slices run as Workflows in fresh worktrees from origin/main on Opus 5.5. Adversarial review is Codex Sol 6.1 at xhigh (user instruction, overriding the standing high). A Sonnet 5.5 CI monitor merges a PR with a merge commit only when every check on the exact head SHA is green, no review thread is unresolved, no bot P1/P2 is open on the tip and the branch is CLEAN (else update-branch and wait); otherwise it messages the PR's owner agent. D-02 still holds and D-128's criteria are kept.
Reason
Desktop "Auto-fix pull requests" cannot be toggled from the session, so autofix means owner agents fixing on the monitor's message.

D-195 One public total Audit.count/0 with a server-side count

Reason

Reason

D-227 PR #209 final-round P3: the totality property allows malformed display names

D-236 Buffer, not drop or fail

When
2026-11-01, #210
Context
Every emit/3 runs after its state change and after any CapStore grant or revoke or device spawn. publish/3 emits twice around a revoke. remove_device emits from handle_info with no caller.
Options
Drop the record (catch and ignore); fail the caller with a named error; buffer and replay.
Decision
Buffer and replay. LiveOps survives, and the record is written once Audit is back.
Reason
Failing the call cannot be atomic: the grant or revoke has already happened, so an error reply would describe an action that did occur, and the DOWN path has no caller. Dropping is the silent evidence gap D-197 refused. It does not claim Audit's own history survives a restart; Audit is in-memory and a restart still wipes it.

D-237 Watermark over state.events, no separate queue and no overflow cap

When
2026-11-01, #210
Context
LiveOps already keeps every event in state.events, with a monotonic id and the same kind and data it sends to Audit.
Options
A bounded queue with an overflow marker; a cursor (audit_cursor) over the retained events.
Decision
Add audit_cursor. Pending means events with id > cursor, replayed oldest first. There is no separate cap.
Reason
Nothing is dropped while LiveOps lives, so an overflow marker would guard a loss that cannot happen. Memory is bounded exactly as before, which is not at all; that is pre-existing and out of scope.

D-238 Defer only when Audit is not there; a timeout still exits

When
2026-11-01, #210
Context
A GenServer.call timeout means Audit may still process the record later. Replaying it would then duplicate it.
Options
Defer on any exit; defer only on noproc, shutdown, normal or killed and let {:timeout, _} propagate.
Decision
Defer only on not-there exits. Re-raise timeouts and every other exit unchanged.
Reason
The row is about the restart window. Deferring a timeout trades a crash for possible duplicate evidence. A stalled Audit is named out of scope. It does not claim LiveOps survives a stalled Audit.

D-239 New Audit.try_record/2; record/2 stays loud

When
2026-11-01, #210
Context
D-195 added count/0 as one total helper and kept events/0 loud for evidence readers. Some 30 other record/2 call sites exist.
Options
Catch inside LiveOps.emit/3; change record/2 to be total; add Audit.try_record/2.
Decision
Add try_record/2, which returns {:ok, event} or {:error, :unavailable} and checks Process.whereis first. Only LiveOps uses it.
Reason
The catch logic sits next to Audit, as with count/0. No other writer silently changes behaviour, because none of them keeps a replayable copy.

D-240 Mark replayed records audit_deferred: true

When
2026-11-01, #210
Context
A replayed record lands later than its peers from other writers, and its Audit at timestamp is the replay time.
Options

Decision
Add audit_deferred: true to every replayed record's metadata. Records written on time stay byte-identical to today's.
Reason
An evidence reader can tell the record was written late, and liveops_event_id still gives the LiveOps order. It does not claim Audit order or at timestamps match when the events happened.

D-241 reset/0 with pending records leaves a :liveops_audit_gap record

When

Options

Reason
reset is a demo and test API, and refusing it would break test setup. The gap is visible in Audit and in the snapshot counters, so the loss is not silent.

D-247 emit/3 writes to Audit before the DemoHub push

D-248 Deferral tests do not pin an exact flushed count

When
2026-11-01, #210
Options
pin flushed: n (plan) | assert pending plus the record contents (chosen)
Decision
The explicit flush_audit() assertions match pending: 0 (and gap: 0) rather than flushed: 1 / flushed: 2; the tests assert the exact Audit records instead
Reason
The 1 s retry timer starts at the first deferred emit and can fire between restarting Audit and the explicit flush on a slow runner, which would make flushed 0. The resulting Audit records are the same either way.

D-249 Deferred events wait for the gap record

When
2026-11-01, #210
Options
always try events after the gap | wait for the gap first (chosen)
Decision
When an audit_gap is still unwritten, flush_audit does not write deferred events in that pass
Reason
The gap record can only be unwritten because Audit is unavailable, so the events would fail anyway. This also guarantees the gap record is written before any newer events.

D-250 Retry timer after reset, and stale timer messages

When
2026-11-01, #210
Options
plan: cancel only | cancel, clear the stale message and re-arm (chosen)
Decision
reset/0 starts the retry timer when it leaves a gap. If Process.cancel_timer/1 returns false, an already-delivered :flush_audit message is removed from the mailbox.
Reason
Without the timer, a gap left by reset would only be written on the next emit or an explicit flush. Removing the stale message stops a second retry timer from being left running.

D-251 Tests filter Audit records to LiveOps ones

Decision
Tests that count all Audit events only look at records that carry liveops_event_id
Reason
This keeps the tests from breaking if some other process writes to Audit during the test.

D-253 Bounded audit replay batch

When
2026-11-01, #210
Options
Drain everything in one run (the old blocking behaviour); bounded batch on the 1 s timer (slow); bounded batch with an immediate continuation (chosen)
Decision
Batch size 500. After a full batch, continue at once with send_after 0, replacing any pending 1 s retry. Keep the 1 s retry only when Audit is unavailable.
Reason
This keeps each handler run short, so callers do not hit the 5 s GenServer.call timeout. A healthy backlog still drains without waiting on the timer.

D-254 reset/0 drains fully before recording a gap

When
2026-11-01, #210
Options
One batch then gap (loses records); full drain (chosen)
Decision
handle_call(:reset) calls drain_audit/1, which repeats bounded flushes until nothing is pending or a flush makes no progress. Only records Audit cannot accept become the gap.
Reason
With bounded batches alone, a reset against a working Audit would turn records Audit could have taken into a gap. Reset is a destructive control call, so blocking there is acceptable. Covered by the test 'reset with a healthy Audit drains a multi-batch backlog'.

D-255 flushed counts the gap record

When
2026-11-01, #210
Options
Count gap writes (chosen); document that flushed counts only replayed events
Decision
flushed is the number of Audit records this call wrote, including a :liveops_audit_gap record.
Reason
This matches the documented meaning, 'records written now'.

D-256 Round 2 P2: follow-up row instead of a third patch

When
2026-11-01, #210
Context
Round 1 found unbounded replay in flush and the fix bounded it. That fix's reset drain then brought the same unbounded replay back on the reset path.
Options
(a) keep drain + design row; (b) one bounded flush on reset and gap the rest; (c) carry-forward replay state
Decision
Kept drain_audit, documented reset as O(backlog) synchronous, and proposed a design row (row 314)
Reason
This is the same kind of problem a third time (rule 3). Removing the drain (option b) would knowingly drop records a live Audit could have taken, which brings back round 1's data loss. Adding carry-forward state would be new behaviour with no settled memory bound, and that bound is exactly the design question.

Cheap, keeps the exact count assertions, and leaves the 0-delay batch continuation and the default auto-retry test path as they are. The test module is async: false, so put_env is safe.

D-258 PR #210 review: Codex Sol 6.1 xhigh rounds and dispositions

When
2026-11-01, #210
Finding
Round 1: (P2 live_ops.ex:727) replay kept LiveOps busy until the whole backlog was written; (P3 lattice_liveops_test.exs:195) automatic retry had no regression coverage; (P3 live_ops.ex:172) flushed did not count the gap record. Round 2: (P2 live_ops.ex:769) reset restores unbounded synchronous replay through drain_audit; (P3 live_ops.ex:740) a gap write exceeded the documented batch limit (flushed: 501); (P3 lattice_liveops_test.exs:292) the gap-count assertion raced the automatic retry. Final round: (P3 lattice_liveops_test.exs:377) in-flight Audit termination lacks regression coverage: every outage test stops Audit before writing, bypassing try_record/2's exit handler, so removing that handler leaves all 16 tests passing; a probe (suspend Audit, queue a denial write, then kill Audit) crashes LiveOps with that mutation, while HEAD survives and replays the event.
Decision
Round 1: all three fixed. flush_audit/2 writes at most 500 records per run and queues an immediate :flush_audit continuation (send_after 0) when more remain, re-arming the 1 s retry only when Audit is unavailable; the new test 'a large backlog drains in bounded batches' fails with the limit removed (its flushed <= batch check could rarely pass trivially if the timer chain drains first; no 30k-scale timing repro was run). The new test 'an idle LiveOps replays the backlog after Audit returns' fails with Process.send_after disabled. flushed now includes the gap record; the reset test pins flushed: 1. Round 2: the P2 became follow-up row 314 (D-256): it is the same class as round 1's P2, caused by round 1's own drain_audit commit 0a62677e3, so under rule 3 it was not patched a third time; the moduledoc states it as a known residual. The batch-limit P3 is fixed: the event allowance drops to 499 when a call writes the gap record, and the test 'a flush that writes a gap record stays within the batch limit' asserts flushed: 500, pending: 1 (flushed: 501 with the fix reverted). The race P3 is fixed: the retry interval is read at runtime from :lattice_core, :liveops_audit_retry_ms and hold_audit_retry!/0 sets it to 1 h (D-257); a 1.1 s pause reproduced the race (flushed: 0) without the hold and passed with it; the pause was not committed. Final round: the P3 was open on the reviewed tip and is filed as row 315. No P1 was reported.
Reason
One adversarial review, since rule 3 does not apply to this diff (D-245). The round 2 P2 is a design question about the reset contract, not a bug a further patch should guess at. The final P3 is test coverage only; production behaviour on HEAD is correct. No workflow, W4 or Phase G claim.

D-259 Row 315: in-flight Audit termination test

When
2026-11-01, #210
Options
Kill Audit with the write queued in its mailbox and replay through flush_audit/0 with the retry timer held (chosen); rely on the 1 s retry after the restart (races the timer); stop Audit through the supervisor (never exercises the exit handler)
Decision
The test 'Audit killed with a LiveOps write in flight defers the record' suspends Audit with :sys.suspend/1, issues record_denial/4 from a Task, waits until LiveOps' own {:record, :liveops_denied, _} call is in Audit's mailbox, kills Audit, then asserts the Task returns :ok, the LiveOps pid is unchanged, and after the supervisor restarts Audit flush_audit/0 returns flushed: 1, pending: 0 with one audit_deferred record. hold_audit_retry!/0 keeps the timer out of the count.
Reason
It is the only test that reaches try_record/2's exit handler. With the handler removed, this test fails (LiveOps crashes on the :killed exit) and the other 16 still pass. Test coverage only; no production change, and no workflow, W4 or Phase G claim.

D-260 Oldest-first audit queue (PR #210 Codex P2, live_ops.ex:756)

When
2026-11-01, #210
Finding
During an Audit outage every emit calls flush_audit/2, which selected the batch with take_while and reverse over the newest-first state.events, so each emit walked the whole backlog and N outage emits cost O(N^2).
Options
Skip the flush while the retry timer is armed (breaks the next-emit-flushes-first ordering test); oldest-first :queue of unacknowledged events (chosen)
Decision
emit/3 also appends the event to state.audit_queue; write_audit_batch/3 peeks the front, drops it on {:ok, _}, and stops after the allowance or the first {:error, :unavailable}. reset/0's fresh_state/0 empties the queue. Commit 990339d51. The test 'a deferred emit does not scale with the backlog' compares one deferred emit's LiveOps reductions at a 10- and a 5000-event backlog: 152 to 21183 with the old scan reinstated, flat with the queue.
Reason
Work per flush is now bounded by the batch plus one failed write, which is what the moduledoc already claimed. Reductions are deterministic, so the test has no wall-clock threshold. Memory is shared terms, not copies. reset/0's full drain (row 314) is unchanged.

D-261 Row 315 test: stub Audit instead of :sys.suspend

When
2026-11-01, #210
Finding
Flagship run 41384634389 failed 'Audit killed with a LiveOps write in flight defers the record' with a record_denial call timeout, and three later tests failed with it. LiveOps sent its :liveops_denied write about 5 s after the Task's call, so LiveOps was first blocked in an unrelated call into the suspended Audit (in the umbrella run, processes from other apps share the VM).
Options
Longer timeouts (still flaky); quiesce LiveOps before suspending (async DOWN and snapshot callers still race); a stub registered as Lattice.Audit (chosen)
Decision
The test terminates the real Audit, registers a stub that replies to unrelated calls at once and kills itself on LiveOps' :liveops_denied call, then asserts the deferral before restarting Audit and replaying through flush_audit/0. Commit ff0534935. Calling LiveOps.snapshot/0 in a loop during the test reproduced the timeout on the old version; the stub version passes. It still fails with try_record/2's exit handler removed.
Reason
The in-flight kill stays exact and no longer depends on what else in the VM calls Audit. Test change only; no production change.

D-267 A bounded Space admin authorizes a replacement, not a root or custodian quorum

When
2026-11-01, #211
Context
The task prompt said "root/custodian quorum". The adopted contract (section 1) delegates replacement to an honored bounded Space-admin action: the admin's pre-loss ceiling includes replace_catalog_v1, with live: false, a finite lease and an honored R03 beacon. The contract rejects a catalog witness quorum, and the living root's unleased genesis cap cannot authorize.
Options
(1) A root or custodian quorum, as the prompt says. (2) A bounded admin, as the contract says. (3) Both.
Decision
Option 2. R04 witnesses govern acquisition and renewal only. The custodian only proves possession of the new key. Optional co-signing is OD-11, default no.
Reason
Follow the adopted contract over the computed prompt. A malicious current admin can replace transport during its lease; that is a stated residual, not hidden.

D-269 Reuse the possession domain without a discriminator

When
2026-11-01, #211
Context
Rotation possession already signs ['lattice-treehouse-transport-possession-v1', :catalog, rotation_record]. A replacement signs the same domain and purpose over the claim.
Options
(1) Reuse the domain and rely on the closed, differing field sets (the claim has parents and prior_catalogs; a rotation has parent and prior_catalog). (2) Add a kind discriminator.
Decision
Option 1, with a named negative test: rotation possession bytes never verify as replacement possession, and the reverse. Filed as OD-12 for the operator.
Reason
Canonical map encoding includes keys and both records are closed, so the bytes cannot coincide. The contract fixes this domain. No domain separation is claimed beyond the closed grammars.

D-270 Multi-parent binding nodes carry the service identity, origin and refused?

When
2026-11-01, #211; extended in review round 1
Context
A gap the contract leaves open. Today the resolver reads the service from the bootstrap (catalog_trust.ex:566 and :672), and routes/1 (:1349/:1351), TS catalogRoutes (:794-797) and the head/fork/ancestry code (catalog_trust.ex:1288-1351, TS :290-310, :515, :523) are single-parent. A replacement changes the service, so without this the new catalog's entries would be refused.
Decision
Every binding node carries the catalog key, service ID, service key, origin, parents and refused?. Entry, realm and key-distinctness checks, routes, heads, forks and ancestry read the current binding and are multi-parent. Persisted single-parent trust stores (TS :479) still restore after upgrade. Applies in BEAM, TS and (PR 5) native.
Reason
Confirmed against the cited source lines in review round 1. The C10 test asserts that routes dial new_origin.

D-271 prior_catalogs cardinality and key rules; both keys may be kept (OD-15)

When
2026-11-01, #211; revised in review round 1
Context
The first draft pinned exactly one prior_catalogs entry per parent. Contract line 251 does not require that, and the pin made a catalog fork inside one binding impossible to resolve.
Decision
Sorted, distinct {binding, catalog} entries, at least one per parent, covering every retained catalog head; nil only as a sole entry. A changed service key means a changed service ID. A replacement may keep both keys and the service ID, which a fork resolution needs (OD-15); the plan 274 no-op refusal stays rotation-only.
Reason
Each is a choice the contract leaves to R11c review, and the vectors would otherwise be ambiguous. Tests in PR 1 (codec) and PR 4 C12 (a fork inside one binding, resolved).

D-275 Closing rule for superseded bindings (OD-16)

When
2026-11-01, review rounds 1 and 2
Context
Round 1 finding at plan:95: anyone holding the old key could keep rotating a superseded binding and force catalog_fork forever. Round 2: closing only direct parents left ancestor keys able to recreate forks.
Options
Leave the attack as an accepted residual; refuse rotations by arrival order (rejected, order-dependent); the closing rule (chosen).
Decision
Every ancestor-or-self of a parent of any D-276-eligible replacement, honored or refused, admits no old-key rotation or later catalog. Such children are kept as evidence and refused, or reported as authority_changed if already accepted. Admin siblings still fork (C12). Integrator sign-off is required before PR 4 (row 317).
Reason
The node set only grows, so closure only grows: independent of delivery order and stable after reclassification. It reads as an interpretation of the contract, so it is flagged as an operator decision instead of being pinned silently.

D-276 Refused replacement ops are evidence nodes only when authorized at their strict prefix

When
2026-11-01, review rounds 1 and 2
Context
Contract §4 lets a resolution cite a head that is now refused; without refused nodes, recovery after authority_changed is impossible (round 1). Round 2 P1: making every authenticated refused op a node let never-authorized proposals become conflict heads.
Options
Previously-accepted-only (rejected: depends on order across clients); every authenticated op (round-1 text, rejected: the spam attack); strict-prefix admin authority (chosen).
Decision
A replacement that passes every intrinsic check (amended by D-288, OD-17 default: steps 1, 2 including bindings, both possessions and the causal honored bootstrap, and 4–6, step 4 being strict-prefix admin authority) but is refused on the full union is a citable binding node with refused? set; it authorizes nothing. Every other authenticated refused op is raw refusal evidence: exported, never a head or a citable parent, adding no head and no D-275 closure; it counts against parser bounds only.
Reason
An attacker without an admin acquisition cannot pass this test, and it comes from the op's own ancestry, so delivery order does not matter. Spam reduces to the accepted malicious-admin residual (threat row e). D-275 closure is built on the same test, so it only grows.

D-277 Validate the declared graph structurally before comparing with retained evidence

When
2026-11-01, review rounds 1 and 2
Context
Round 1 P1: valid sibling replacements were rejected before fork detection. Round 2: the intrinsic nil-predecessor check was still arrival-dependent, and a cutoff failure masked a retained fork.
Decision
Stage B order: scope, closure, full union, strict prefix, intrinsic declared graph (structural only; an unheld cited catalog is trust_pending; nil is its parent's sole entry), conflict deps, then comparison with retained evidence, where an omitting sibling is catalog_fork, and finally cutoffs (step 8). recovery_incomplete only gates promotion of an otherwise sole valid head and never suppresses a fork record.
Reason
Contract §4 requires this order. It makes verdicts independent of delivery order; PR 4 C12 tests run sequential deliveries in both orders with acceptance persisted between calls.

D-278 A resolution's deps causally include every cited replacement op, not the refusing controls

When
2026-11-01, review round 1; revised in round 3
Context
Round 1 asked for the causal conflict in a resolution's deps. Round 3 P2 (plan:199): requiring every Core control that refused a cited node let a valid late beacon E8 (a descendant of E7, concurrent with R1/R2) invalidate accepted resolution R2.
Options
Keep "every" (rejected: the E8 case); "at least one in closure" (rejected: order-dependent); intrinsic closure-only (chosen).
Decision
Step 6 requires only that the candidate's deps closure includes every cited replacement op. Rotation heads are covered by parents. A cited node's refusal stays a D-276 property of that node.
Reason
Every formulation that reads the union is either unbounded (each later beacon lapses the node again) or depends on delivery order. Lifecycle contract §4 only requires that a resolution cover the observed conflict, and citing the node does that. C13 carries the E7/E8 persisted-state case in both delivery orders.

D-289 Review cutoff for design-only plan PRs

When
2026-11-02, #211 (decided by main at bot review round 4)
Decision
Round 4 is the final fix round for a design-only plan PR. Every open P1 is fixed in the plan. A P2 is fixed only if it takes a one- or two-line correction; otherwise it goes to a short "Open review findings" list, a new index row owned by the build PR, or an operator decision with a recommended default. Each thread is answered with where its finding went and then resolved, and no further round starts. On #211 the four round-4 P2s were each a one- or two-line fix and were fixed: PR 4 is gated on OD-17; step 5 checks the service ID against each parent; PR 1 tests only generation shape, with the max + 1 relation moved to PR 4; step 4 compares both profile_genesis and profile_id at the strict prefix. C11 gains a negative for each.
Reason
Findings on a plan compound from round to round, and each build PR gets its own adversarial review against real code, which is where they get settled (rule 3 spirit). The plan stays at 400 lines (rule 4).

D-301 The display contract covers text fields only

When
2026-11-01, #212
Context
Row 311 says every {:ok, text} is valid UTF-8, but dview issuer/audience are {:ok, pub} with 32 raw key bytes, which are almost never valid UTF-8.
Options
Contract on every {:ok, _} (fails on honest logs) / contract on text fields only
Decision
The contract covers roles/ops members, {:name, _, text} and the revoke {:ok, text}. Keys are {:ok, pubkey}, and a reader must fingerprint them before display. Raw terms in :malformed, :request, :command and :other must be inspected.
Reason
A contract that honest logs break would be wrong. It does not claim that raw terms or keys are display text.

D-302 Move the revoke check into the parser, superseding D-218

When
2026-11-01, #212
Context
D-218 checked only the 8-character prefix the renderer shows, so earlier labels did not move. The parser cannot know the display width, so moving the check means validating the whole target.
Options
Whole-target String.valid? in parse/1 / parser returns a truncated prefix / leave the check in render
Decision
parse/1 returns {:revoke, {:malformed, t}} for a binary target that is not valid UTF-8, and render drops its check. The pinned test 'revoke label checks only the shown prefix' is replaced by one that expects 'revoke (malformed)' for "12345678" <> <<255>>.
Reason
Row 311 asks for this move, and display width is a renderer concern. The only label that changes is an attacker-shaped binary with a valid prefix and an invalid tail. Honest delegation ids are base64url, and the demo artifact diff gate checks that honest labels stay the same. It does not claim the target is a real delegation id.

D-303 Toolshed is covered for totality, not for Jason encoding

When
2026-11-01, #212
Context
Row 311 lists 'the Toolshed model' as a Jason-encodable output. Toolshed.ReadModel returns custody.holder as raw key bytes and request payloads as raw terms (tuples), even on an honest log. No code JSON-encodes it.
Options
Jason on the whole model (fails on honest logs) / change the Toolshed model shape (product work, frozen) / totality plus valid fingerprint strings
Decision
The new property asserts that Toolshed.ReadModel.observe/1 never raises on admitted attacker-shaped authority bodies and that its fingerprint fields are valid UTF-8. Request ref/payload text safety is a candidate residual row (324+).
Reason
The model is not a JSON surface, and its raw fields come from :inbox ops and state, not from authority bodies. It does not claim the Toolshed model is display-safe.

D-304 Make the encodability property check bytes, not types

When
2026-11-01, #212
Context
The row 309 property asserted is_binary on mermaid/dot exports, which is also true for invalid UTF-8, and only is_map on replay.
Options
Keep is_binary / String.valid? and Jason checks on each output
Decision
Layer 1 adds String.valid? to every parsed text field. Layer 2 checks Jason.encode! on trust_graph, op_dag and replay, Jason.decode! on the ReplicaSnapshot json, and String.valid? on the ReplicaSnapshot mermaid/dot and on the Export dot/mermaid bytes AuditBundle writes.
Reason
This is the 'readers that copy text out verbatim' gap the row names. It covers only bodies Sim admits, as D-214 says.

D-305 What counts as an omitted delegation

When
2026-11-01, #212
Context
delegation_events/1 drops ill-formed delegations, malformed-role transfer/succeed, and every non-delegation body in one catch-all clause.
Options
Count every dropped authority op / count only delegation-carrying bodies
Decision
Count an authority op as omitted when it parses as genesis, grant, transfer or succeed but has a malformed role or a dview that fails renderable?/1. Heartbeat, revoke, request, command and other bodies do not count. The shape is omitted: [op_id] in topo order, and an honest log has [].
Reason
Only delegation-carrying ops could have drawn an edge, so only they make the graph look complete when they are dropped. The marker says these ops are present in the log but not drawn. It does not claim the log is complete, which AuditBundle already disclaims.

D-306 Emit the marker in Lattice.Graph.Export, only when non-empty

When
2026-11-01, #212
Context
Export.export/2 reads only nodes and edges, so a new map key alone would leave trust_graph.dot and .mermaid looking complete.
Options
An optional :omitted key read by Export / AuditBundle appends a line / a synthetic graph node
Decision
Export reads Map.get(snapshot, :omitted, []) and, when it is non-empty, writes one comment line after the header ('//' in DOT, '%%' in mermaid) with the count and op ids.
Reason
Callers without the key (v1 snapshot, mix lattice.graph.snapshot, flagship, fault_scenario, ReplicaSnapshot) and honest Township logs stay byte-identical. A synthetic node would break AuditBundle.known_fingerprints/1 label validation. The township-audit-bundle-v1 schema is not bumped, because verify re-derives the same bytes from matter.log.

D-317 PR #212 review: Codex Sol 6.1 rounds and dispositions

When
2026-11-01, #212
Finding
Round 1 (gpt-6.1-sol, reasoning high, commit d9db02c7f): no correctness bugs; read-only probes passed 16 classification cases, topo ordering for concurrent and causal omissions, and a check that op ids are base64url so they cannot inject into the comment line. Final round (P3, apps/lattice_core/test/lattice2/authority_body_test.exs:217): the new Toolshed property bypasses the parser it should exercise. Setup never transfers custody and generated ops are all :authority, so holder and loan stay nil, requests are empty, fingerprints == [nil], and the UTF-8 assertion checks nothing; Toolshed's only AuthorityBody consumer, loan/3, never runs. Codex confirmed that removing its parser guard in memory leaves the property green. D-352 prior_catalogs order and binding-set equality

When
2026-11-02, #214
Context
The plan requires sorted, distinct {binding, catalog} entries, at least one per parent, and nil only as a sole entry. It does not say where nil sorts, or whether an entry may name a non-parent binding.
Options
Tolerate extra bindings; or require the entry binding set to equal parents. Sort nil first or last.
Decision
Strict order by binding, then catalog, with nil before any ID (Erlang term order, mirrored with compareUtf8 in TS). The set of entry bindings must equal parents; an extra binding is malformed_catalog.
Reason
A closed codec should not carry entries that no check reads. Nil-first matches BEAM's natural term order, so both runtimes sort one way. Coverage of retained catalog heads stays a PR 2/PR 4 obligation.

D-353 Parser bounds on claim lists

When
2026-11-02, #214
Context
The claim lists parents, prior_catalogs, deps and epoch_basis have no stated bounds. Head budget overflow (control_history_limit) is a resolver concern.
Options
Leave them unbounded; or set closed codec bounds.
Decision
parents 1..16, prior_catalogs 1..32, deps 1..64, epoch_basis 1..64; cutoffs keep the existing 1..13.
Reason
A closed codec must bound its input. The values sit well above the head budget (two) and the 13-entry catalog, so no valid claim is refused. The resolver's head limit is unchanged. These are codec ceilings, not policy.

D-354 TS decoder returns null, never throws

When
2026-11-02, #214
Context
In TS, a decoder that throws yields malformed_command. The bootstrap decoder returns null and lets spaceStatus refuse with application_invalid_catalog. BEAM refuses with application_invalid_catalog from the Stage A clause.
Options
A throwing decoder; or the null-args bootstrap pattern.
Decision
Follow the catalog_bootstrap_v1 pattern: a malformed claim or a non-binary possession decodes to null, and Stage A refuses with application_invalid_catalog. A parity test pins the same reason in both runtimes.
Reason
Without this, BEAM and TS would quarantine one frame with different reasons.

D-368 Where to fix the TS high-integer bypass

When
2026-11-02, #214
Options
(a) per-command opaque context in decodeCarrierBody; (b) opaque sentinel for all command args; (c) catch in the replacement decoder (impossible, because the throw comes first).
Decision

Reason
The throw happens in the generic body decoder before any command decoder runs, so the replacement decoder cannot catch it. Limiting the change to replace_catalog_v1 avoids changing refusal reasons for other commands whose BEAM behaviour has not been checked. The general decision is row 336.

D-375 Native fails closed on replace_catalog_v1 until PR 5

When
2026-11-02, #214
Finding
An automated security review of the earlier commit 13d9d0645 flagged that native clients/treehouse-tauri-shell/src-tauri/src/catalog_eval added replace_catalog_v1 to the command table with no space_policy Stage A check (fail-open) and no space_projection arm (an unreachable! panic).
Decision
The finding applied to 13d9d0645 only. The branch reverted every native change (D-361), and git diff origin/main -- clients/treehouse-tauri-shell/src-tauri/src is empty at HEAD. Native fails closed: it refuses replace_catalog_v1 as unknown_command (commands.rs:89) before Space policy or projection. This holds until plan 316's native mirror PR (row 319, PR 5, after plan 267). BEAM/TS admit the command to Stage A while native refuses it. This is a known, fail-closed parity gap, not a fail-open one. PR 5 must add both a space_policy Stage A check and a space_projection arm, and must replace unreachable!("closed Space command table") with an error return. The new cargo test native_refuses_replace_catalog_v1_as_unknown_command_without_panicking (tests/catalog_eval_commands.rs) runs the valid claim and all 16 Stage A frames from replacement_v1.json through native preflight, SpacePolicy and space_projection::project. It asserts unknown_command at every layer with no panic. Adding only a table entry makes it fail (checked by mutation).
Reason
Packaged clients must not accept a replacement that BEAM and TS would refuse. A refusal they all share is safe. A native acceptance with no Stage A, or a panic, is not.

D-376 PR 1 flips the PR 0 tripwire to :application_invalid_catalog

When
2026-11-02, #214
Context

Stage A then refuses the probe's stub claim and zero possessions. T5(a) now asserts :application_invalid_catalog from both Sim.quarantined/3 and Authority.analyze/2, and the op stays in every realm's authenticated log. T5(b) is unchanged.

D-451 Key by value, keep the helper and one ledger per ceremony directory

Context

D-453 Replacement revision 0 has its own genesis rule

When
2026-11-02, #217
Context
extends?(catalog, nil) accepts only entry kinds exactly [:space, :thread]. Under a replacement binding, revision 0 carries the whole inventory, its inventory_id must equal claim.inventory_digest, and every entry carries the new service ID and key (plan 316 §2, D-268).
Options

Reason
The custodian must not sign a revision 0 that a retained client would refuse, and the claim fixes the inventory. It does not check prior-catalog coverage or cutoffs, which are the resolver's (PR 4).

D-454 Binding.replacement/3 derives, it does not trust

D-467 Per-binding envelope bound instead of a global raise to 13
